To fix (3)

To fix Exact P-Values

What we found

We found 2 imprecise p values out of 50 detected p values.

Show details

Reporting p values imprecisely (e.g., p < .05) reduces transparency, reproducibility, and re-use (e.g., in p value meta-analyses). Best practice is to report exact p-values with three decimal places (e.g., p = .032) unless p values are smaller than 0.001, in which case you can use p < .001.

This module sometimes flags false positives when sentences discuss how p-value thresholds were set. It can also miss an imprecise p-value that is reported against a threshold (e.g., “decreased at the p < .05 significance threshold”).

P-Value Text
p > .05 Baseline characteristics were comparable across conditions (all p > .05; Table 1), except for subjective socioeconomic status (SES), which differed when analyzed categorically (χ²(4) = 23.74, p < .001), but not as an ordinal score (Mann-Whitney p = .10).
p ≤ .010 Adjusting for SES did not materially change the primary treatment effects (all p ≤ .010; Supplementary Note 2).

The APA manual states: Report exact p values (e.g., p = .031) to two or three decimal places. However, report p values less than .001 as p < .001. However, 2 decimals is too imprecise for many use-cases (e.g., a p value meta-analysis), so report p values with three digits.

American Psychological Association (2020). Publication manual of the American Psychological Association, 7 edition. American Psychological Association.

Spreadsheet Formatting

File Sheet Issue Detail
Data_Dictionary.xlsx Data Dictionary Merged cells 6 merged ranges (A2:F2, A38:F38, A10:F10, A33:F33, A36:F36) break the rectangular grid.

Spreadsheet formatting such as colour, merged cells, and blank rows/columns is not preserved when data are read programmatically or exported to CSV. Store data as a plain rectangular table (one header row, one column per variable, no colour-encoded meaning) so it is machine-readable.

Careless Responding

No file could be screened for careless responding.

What this does not cover. Only 0 of 4 data files could be screened: the rest had no detectable multi-item scale block, or too few respondents. Even in a screened file, these checks only look at multi-item rating scales — the only place where a repeated or flat answer pattern is interpretable. Single questions, open text, demographics and any item outside a detected scale block are never examined. A respondent can therefore answer most of a survey carelessly and still not be flagged here, and finding nothing is not evidence that a dataset is free of careless responding.

To check (4)

To check Non-Significant P Value Check

What we found

We found 15 non-significant p values that should be checked for appropriate interpretation.

Show details

Meta-scientific research has shown nonsignificant p values are commonly misinterpreted. It is incorrect to infer that there is ‘no effect’, ‘no difference’, or that groups are ‘the same’ after p > 0.05.

It is possible that there is a true non-zero effect, but that the study did not detect it. Make sure your inference acknowledges that it is possible that there is a non-zero effect. It is correct to include the effect is ‘not significantly’ different, although this just restates that p > 0.05.

Metacheck does not yet analyze automatically whether sentences which include non-significant p-values are correct, but we recommend manually checking the sentences below for possible misinterpreted non-significant p values.

For metascientific articles demonstrating the rate of misinterpretations of non-significant results is high, see:

Aczel B, Palfi B, Szollosi A, Kovacs M, Szaszi B, Szecsi P, Zrubka M, Gronau Q, van den Bergh D, Wagenmakers E (2018). “Quantifying Support for the Null Hypothesis in Psychology: An Empirical Investigation.” Advances in Methods and Practices in Psychological Science, 1(3), 357–366. doi:10.1177/2515245918773742.

Murphy S, Merz R, Reimann L, Fernández A (2025). “Nonsignificance misinterpreted as an effect’s absence in psychology: Prevalence and temporal analyses.” Royal Society Open Science, 12(3), 242167. doi:10.1098/rsos.242167.

For educational material on preventing the misinterpretation of p values, see Improving Your Statistical Inferences.

README Files

README files are a way to document the contents and structure of a folder, helping users locate the information they need. You can use a README to document changes to a repository, and explain how files are named. Please consider adding a README to each repository or including ‘README’ in the name of your overview document.

Code Comments

Best programming practice is to add comments to code, to explain what the code does (to yourself in the future, or peers who want to re-use your code). All your code files had comments.

Missing Files

All files loaded in the code were present in the repository.

Absolute Paths

Best programming practice is to use relative file paths (e.g., ‘./files’) instead of absolute file paths (e.g., ‘C://Lakens/project_dir/files’) as these folder names do not exist on other computers. No absolute file paths were found in any of the code files.

Working Directory (setwd)

Best programming practice is to avoid setwd() in analysis code: it hardcodes an assumption about the working directory (often an absolute path on the author’s own machine), so the code breaks when run anywhere else. Keep the working directory as the caller sets it and use relative paths. No setwd() calls were found in any of the code files.

Package Installation (install.packages)

Best programming practice is to avoid install.packages() in analysis code: it installs software on whoever runs the script without asking, which is at minimum disruptive (an unexpected install, network access, a package version the author never tested against) and at worst a security risk if it points at an untrusted repository. Document required packages instead (a README, or a list of them: an renv.lock/DESCRIPTION for R, a requirements.txt for Python). No install.packages() calls were found in any of the code files.

Libraries / Imports

Best programming practice is to load all required libraries/imports in one block near the top of the code. In all code files, libraries/imports were loaded in one block.

Packages / Dependencies

The code files load 18 distinct packages/libraries: broom, car, conflicted, dplyr, effectsize, grid, knitr, lavaan, lme4, lmerTest, lubridate, parameters, psych, purrr, stringr, tibble, tidyr, tidyverse. These are the names found in the source (no version information is available from static analysis).

Reproducible Environment

No renv.lock file, sessionInfo()/session_info() record, or groundhog/checkpoint date-pin call was found anywhere in the repository. Without one of these, the exact R and package versions used for the analysis are not recoverable, and package versions may drift between when this analysis was run and any later reproduction attempt. Consider depositing an renv.lock (via the renv package), a sessionInfo() text dump, or a groundhog/checkpoint date-pin alongside the code.

> extract_lmer_term <- function(model, term_name) {
>   
>   coef_table <- as.data.frame(summary(model)$coefficients)
>   coef_table$term <- rownames(coef_table)
>   
>   conf <- as.data.frame(confint(model, parm = "beta_", method = "Wald"))
>   conf$term <- rownames(conf)
>   names(conf)[1:2] <- c("LL", "UL")

>   coef_table |>
>     dplyr::filter(term == term_name) |>
>     dplyr::left_join(conf, by = "term") |>
>     dplyr::transmute(
>       term,
>       b = Estimate,
>       SE = `Std. Error`,
>       df = df,
>       t = `t value`,
>       p = `Pr(>|t|)`,
>       LL,
>       UL
>     )
> }

> run_subgroup_lmm <- function(data, outcome, baseline_cat_var) {
>   groups <- levels(data[[baseline_cat_var]])
> 
>   map_dfr(groups, function(group_label) {
>     d <- data |>
>       filter(.data[[baseline_cat_var]] == group_label) |>
>       filter(
>         !is.na(.data[[outcome]]),
>         !is.na(cond),
>         !is.na(time),
>         !is.na(unique_ID),
>         !is.na(term)
>       ) |>
>       mutate(
>         cond = factor(cond, levels = c("Control", "Flourish")),
>         term = factor(term),
>         outcome_z = as.numeric(scale(.data[[outcome]])),
>         time_z = as.numeric(scale(time - 2.5))
>       )
> 
>     contrasts(d$cond) <- cbind(
>       flourish_vs_control = c(-1, 1)
>     )
> 
>     raw_formula <- as.formula(
>       paste0(
>         outcome,
>         " ~ cond * I(time - 2.5) + ",
>         "(1 | unique_ID) + (1 | term)"
>       )
>     )
> 
>     raw_fit <- lmer(
>       raw_formula,
>       data = d,
>       REML = TRUE
>     )
> 
>     raw <- extract_lmer_term(
>       raw_fit,
>       "condflourish_vs_control:I(time - 2.5)"
>     )
> 
>     std_fit <- lmer(
>       outcome_z ~ cond * time_z +
>         (1 | unique_ID) +
>         (1 | term),
>       data = d,
>       REML = TRUE
>     )
> 
>     std <- extract_lmer_term(
>       std_fit,
>       "condflourish_vs_control:time_z"
>     ) |>
>       transmute(
>         beta = b,
>         beta_LL = LL,
>         beta_UL = UL
>       )
> 
>     bind_cols(raw, std) |>
>       mutate(baseline_group = group_label)
>   })
> }

>   mutate(
>     time = as.numeric(time),
>     week = case_when(time == 1 ~ 0, time == 2 ~ 2, time == 3 ~ 4, time == 4 ~ 6),
>     time_c = time - 2.5,
>     time_f = factor(time, levels = c(1, 2, 3, 4), labels = c("Baseline", "Week 2", "Week 4", "Week 6"))
>   ) |>
>   group_by(unique_ID) |>
>   fill(cond, Sex, Age, starts_with("Ethnicity"), int_student, int_student_country, SES, SES_num, term, .direction = "downup") |>
>   mutate(
>     depression_baseline = depression[time == 1][1],
>     anxiety_baseline = anxiety[time == 1][1],
>     perceived_stress_baseline = perceived_stress[time == 1][1],
>     # midpoint split for the PSS-4
>     perceived_stress_baseline_cat = case_when(
>       perceived_stress_baseline >= 8 ~ "Elevated baseline (PSS-4 ≥ 8)",
>       !is.na(perceived_stress_baseline) ~ "Low baseline (PSS-4 < 8)",
>       TRUE ~ NA_character_
>     ),
>     depression_baseline_cat = case_when(
>       depression_baseline >= 3 ~ "Elevated baseline (PHQ-2 ≥ 3)",
>       !is.na(depression_baseline) ~ "Low baseline (PHQ-2 < 3)",
>       TRUE ~ NA_character_
>     ),
>     anxiety_baseline_cat = case_when(
>       anxiety_baseline >= 3 ~ "Elevated baseline (GAD-2 ≥ 3)",
>       !is.na(anxiety_baseline) ~ "Low baseline (GAD-2 < 3)",
>       TRUE ~ NA_character_
>     )
>   ) |>
>   ungroup() |>
>   mutate(
>     cond = case_when(
>       str_to_lower(as.character(cond)) == "control" ~ "Control",
>       str_to_lower(as.character(cond)) == "flourish" ~ "Flourish",
>       TRUE ~ as.character(cond)
>     ),
>     cond = factor(cond, levels = c("Control", "Flourish")),
>     term = factor(term),
>     perceived_stress_baseline_cat = factor(
>       perceived_stress_baseline_cat,
>       levels = c(
>         "Low baseline (PSS-4 < 8)",
>         "Elevated baseline (PSS-4 ≥ 8)"
>       )
>     ),
>     depression_baseline_cat = factor(depression_baseline_cat, levels = c("Low baseline (PHQ-2 < 3)", "Elevated baseline (PHQ-2 ≥ 3)")),
>     anxiety_baseline_cat = factor(anxiety_baseline_cat, levels = c("Low baseline (GAD-2 < 3)", "Elevated baseline (GAD-2 ≥ 3)")),
>     Ethnicity_WhitePOC = factor(if_else(Ethnicity_categ == "White", "White", "Non-White", missing = NA_character_))
>   )
> contrasts(merged_data_long$cond) <- cbind(flourish_vs_control = c(-1, 1))
> data_baseline <- merged_data_long |>
>   filter(time == 1) |>
>   distinct(unique_ID, .keep_all = TRUE) |>
>   mutate(
>     perceived_stress_baseline_severity = case_when(
>       perceived_stress >= 8 ~ "Stress Elevated (PSS-4 ≥ 8)",
>       perceived_stress < 8 ~ "Stress Low (PSS-4 < 8)",

>     ),

> make_cat_rows <- function(data, var, label, levels_to_show = NULL, include_missing = TRUE) {
>   if (is.null(levels_to_show)) levels_to_show <- sort(unique(na.omit(data[[var]])))
> 
>   out <- map_dfr(levels_to_show, function(lvl) {
>     control_n <- sum(data$cond == "Control" & data[[var]] == lvl, na.rm = TRUE)
>     flourish_n <- sum(data$cond == "Flourish" & data[[var]] == lvl, na.rm = TRUE)
>     total_n <- sum(data[[var]] == lvl, na.rm = TRUE)
> 
>     tibble(
>       Characteristic = paste0("     ", lvl),
>       Control = fmt_n_pct(control_n, sum(data$cond == "Control")),
>       Flourish = fmt_n_pct(flourish_n, sum(data$cond == "Flourish")),
>       Total = fmt_n_pct(total_n, nrow(data))
>     )
>   })
> 
>   if (include_missing) {
>     out <- bind_rows(
>       out,
>       tibble(
>         Characteristic = "     Missing",
>         Control = fmt_n_pct(sum(data$cond == "Control" & is.na(data[[var]])), sum(data$cond == "Control")),
>         Flourish = fmt_n_pct(sum(data$cond == "Flourish" & is.na(data[[var]])), sum(data$cond == "Flourish")),
>         Total = fmt_n_pct(sum(is.na(data[[var]])), nrow(data))
>       )
>     )
>   }
> 
>   if (!is.null(label)) {
>     bind_rows(tibble(Characteristic = label, Control = "", Flourish = "", Total = ""), out)
>   } else {
>     out
>   }
> }

> rand_anx <- lm(anxiety ~ cond, data = data_baseline_check)

> monotone_observed <- function(x) {
>   out <- x
>   running_max <- -Inf
> 
>   for (i in seq_along(x)) {
>     if (!is.na(x[i])) {
>       running_max <- max(running_max, x[i])
>       out[i] <- running_max
>     }
>   }
> 
>   out
> }
> max_or_na <- function(x) {
>   if (all(is.na(x))) {
>     NA_real_
>   } else {
>     max(x, na.rm = TRUE)
>   }
> }
> df_fl <- merged_data_long |>
>   filter(
>     cond == "Flourish",
>     time %in% c(2, 3, 4)
>   ) |>
>   select(
>     unique_ID,
>     time,
>     Engagement_1,
>     Engagement_3
>   ) |>
>   mutate(
>     max_possible_days = case_when(
>       time == 2 ~ 14,
>       time == 3 ~ 28,
>       time == 4 ~ 42
>     ),
> 
>     impossible_active_days =
>       !is.na(Engagement_1) &
>       (Engagement_1 < 0 | Engagement_1 > max_possible_days),
> 
>     impossible_activities =
>       !is.na(Engagement_3) &
>       Engagement_3 < 0,
> 
>     Engagement_1_clean = case_when(
>       is.na(Engagement_1) ~ NA_real_,
>       Engagement_1 < 0 ~ 0,
>       Engagement_1 > max_possible_days ~ max_possible_days,
>       TRUE ~ Engagement_1
>     ),
> 
>     Engagement_3_clean = case_when(
>       is.na(Engagement_3) ~ NA_real_,
>       Engagement_3 < 0 ~ 0,
>       TRUE ~ Engagement_3
>     )
>   ) |>
>   arrange(unique_ID, time)
> df_fl_fix <- df_fl |>
>   group_by(unique_ID) |>
>   mutate(
>     Engagement_1_fix = monotone_observed(Engagement_1_clean),
>     Engagement_3_fix = monotone_observed(Engagement_3_clean)
>   ) |>
>   ungroup()
> engagement_qc <- df_fl_fix |>
>   summarise(

>     impossible_activity_entries = sum(impossible_activities, na.rm = TRUE),

> get_slopes_with_beta <- function(data, outcome) {
>   
>   map_dfr(c("Control", "Flourish"), function(condition_label) {
>     
>     d <- data |>
>       filter(cond == condition_label) |>
>       filter(
>         !is.na(.data[[outcome]]),
>         !is.na(time),
>         !is.na(unique_ID),
>         !is.na(term)
>       ) |>
>       mutate(
>         outcome_z = as.numeric(scale(.data[[outcome]])),
>         time_z = as.numeric(scale(time - 2.5))
>       )
>     
>     # Unstandardized model
>     raw_fit <- lmer(
>       reformulate(
>         termlabels = c("I(time - 2.5)", "(1 | unique_ID)", "(1 | term)"),
>         response = outcome
>       ),
>       data = d
>     )
>     
>     raw <- extract_lmer_term(raw_fit, "I(time - 2.5)") |>
>       mutate(cond = condition_label)
>     
>     # Manually standardized model
>     std_fit <- lmer(
>       outcome_z ~ time_z + (1 | unique_ID) + (1 | term),
>       data = d
>     )
>     
>     std <- extract_lmer_term(std_fit, "time_z") |>
>       transmute(
>         cond = condition_label,
>         beta = b,
>         beta_LL = LL,
>         beta_UL = UL
>       )
>     
>     raw |>
>       left_join(std, by = "cond")
>   }) |>
>     mutate(
>       across(
>         c(b, SE, t, LL, UL, beta, beta_LL, beta_UL),
>         ~ round(.x, 2)
>       ),
>       p_formatted = format_p(p)
>     )
> }
> stress_slopes <- get_slopes_with_beta(merged_data_long, "perceived_stress")
> depression_slopes <- get_slopes_with_beta(merged_data_long, "depression")
> anxiety_slopes <- get_slopes_with_beta(merged_data_long, "anxiety")
> stress_slopes |> kable(caption = "Perceived-stress slopes over time by condition")

> get_condition_diffs_original <- function(data, outcome) {
>   map_dfr(1:4, function(tt) {
>     fit <- lmer(
>       as.formula(paste0(outcome, " ~ cond + (1 | term)")),
>       data = data |> filter(time == tt)
>     )
>     extract_lmer_term(fit, "condflourish_vs_control") |>
>       transmute(
>         time = tt,
>         time_f = factor(tt, levels = c(1, 2, 3, 4), labels = c("Baseline", "Week 2", "Week 4", "Week 6")),
>         b, SE, df, t, p, LL, UL
>       )
>   }) |>
>     mutate(p_formatted = format_p(p))
> }

Table: Anxiety condition × time × baseline symptom severity interaction

|term                                                                                    |     b|   SE|       df|     t|         p|    LL|   UL|p_formatted |
|:---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----:|----:|--------:|-----:|---------:|-----:|----:|:-----------|
|condflourish_vs_control:I(time - 2.5):anxiety_baseline_catElevated baseline (GAD-2 ≥ 3) | -0.01| 0.03| 3070.955| -0.25| 0.7989183| -0.08| 0.06|0.799       |
> format_ci_bound <- function(x) {
>   case_when(
>     is.na(x) ~ NA_character_,
>     x != 0 & abs(x) < .01 ~ sprintf("%.3f", x),
>     TRUE ~ sprintf("%.2f", x)
>   )
> }
> format_standardized_effect <- function(symbol, estimate, lower, upper) {
>   paste0(
>     symbol,
>     " = ",
>     format_ci_bound(estimate),
>     " (",
>     format_ci_bound(lower),
>     " to ",
>     format_ci_bound(upper),
>     ")"
>   )
> }
> run_subgroup_trajectory <- function(
>     data,
>     outcome,
>     baseline_cat_var,
>     subgroup_label
> ) {
> 
>   d <- data |>
>     filter(.data[[baseline_cat_var]] == subgroup_label) |>
>     filter(
>       !is.na(.data[[outcome]]),
>       !is.na(cond),
>       !is.na(time),
>       !is.na(unique_ID),
>       !is.na(term)
>     ) |>
>     mutate(
>       cond = factor(cond, levels = c("Control", "Flourish")),
>       term = factor(term),
>       outcome_z = as.numeric(scale(.data[[outcome]])),
>       time_z = as.numeric(scale(time - 2.5))
>     )
> 
>   contrasts(d$cond) <- cbind(
>     flourish_vs_control = c(-1, 1)
>   )
> 
>   # 1. Subgroup-specific condition × time interaction
> 
>   raw_formula <- reformulate(
>     termlabels = c(
>       "cond * I(time - 2.5)",
>       "(1 | unique_ID)",
>       "(1 | term)"
>     ),
>     response = outcome
>   )
> 
>   fit <- lmer(
>     raw_formula,
>     data = d,
>     REML = TRUE
>   )
> 
>   raw_interaction <- extract_lmer_term(
>     fit,
>     "condflourish_vs_control:I(time - 2.5)"
>   )
> 
>   std_interaction_fit <- lmer(
>     outcome_z ~ cond * time_z +
>       (1 | unique_ID) +
>       (1 | term),
>     data = d,
>     REML = TRUE
>   )
> 
>   std_interaction <- extract_lmer_term(
>     std_interaction_fit,
>     "condflourish_vs_control:time_z"
>   ) |>
>     transmute(
>       beta = b,
>       beta_LL = LL,
>       beta_UL = UL
>     )
> 
>   interaction <- bind_cols(
>     raw_interaction,
>     std_interaction
>   ) |>
>     mutate(
>       outcome = .env$outcome,
>       subgroup = subgroup_label
>     )
> 
>   # 2. Within-condition slopes
> 
>   slopes <- map_dfr(
>     c("Control", "Flourish"),
>     function(condition_label) {
> 
>       d_condition <- d |>
>         filter(cond == condition_label) |>
>         mutate(
>           outcome_condition_z = as.numeric(
>             scale(.data[[outcome]])
>           ),
>           time_condition_z = as.numeric(
>             scale(time - 2.5)
>           )
>         )
> 
>       raw_slope_formula <- reformulate(
>         termlabels = c(
>           "I(time - 2.5)",
>           "(1 | unique_ID)",
>           "(1 | term)"
>         ),
>         response = outcome
>       )
> 
>       fit_condition <- lmer(
>         raw_slope_formula,
>         data = d_condition,
>         REML = TRUE
>       )
> 
>       raw <- extract_lmer_term(
>         fit_condition,
>         "I(time - 2.5)"
>       ) |>
>         mutate(cond = condition_label)
> 
>       std_condition_fit <- lmer(
>         outcome_condition_z ~ time_condition_z +
>           (1 | unique_ID) +
>           (1 | term),
>         data = d_condition,
>         REML = TRUE
>       )
> 
>       std <- extract_lmer_term(
>         std_condition_fit,
>         "time_condition_z"
>       ) |>
>         transmute(
>           cond = condition_label,
>           beta = b,
>           beta_LL = LL,
>           beta_UL = UL
>         )
> 
>       raw |>
>         left_join(std, by = "cond")
>     }
>   ) |>
>     mutate(
>       outcome = .env$outcome,
>       subgroup = subgroup_label
>     )
> 
>   # 3. Between-condition differences at each timepoint
> 
>   timepoint_diffs <- map_dfr(
>     1:4,
>     function(tt) {
> 
>       d_time <- d |>
>         filter(time == tt)
> 
>       fit_time <- lmer(
>         reformulate(
>           termlabels = c("cond", "(1 | term)"),
>           response = outcome
>         ),
>         data = d_time,
>         REML = TRUE
>       )
> 
>       extract_lmer_term(
>         fit_time,
>         "condflourish_vs_control"
>       ) |>
>         transmute(
>           time = tt,
>           time_f = factor(
>             tt,
>             levels = c(1, 2, 3, 4),
>             labels = c(
>               "Baseline",
>               "Week 2",
>               "Week 4",
>               "Week 6"
>             )
>           ),
>           b,
>           SE,
>           df,
>           t,
>           p,
>           LL,
>           UL,
>           outcome = .env$outcome,
>           subgroup = subgroup_label
>         )
>     }
>   )
> 
>   # 4. Week 6 between-condition Cohen's d
> 
>   week6_data <- d |>
>     filter(time == 4) |>
>     transmute(
>       cond,
>       value = .data[[outcome]]
>     ) |>
>     filter(!is.na(value))
> 
>   week6_d <- effectsize::cohens_d(
>     value ~ cond,
>     data = week6_data
>   ) |>
>     as_tibble() |>
>     transmute(
>       outcome = .env$outcome,
>       subgroup = subgroup_label,
>       d = Cohens_d,
>       d_LL = CI_low,
>       d_UL = CI_high
>     )
> 
>   list(
>     interaction = interaction,
>     slopes = slopes,
>     timepoint_diffs = timepoint_diffs,
>     week6_d = week6_d
>   )
> }
> stress_low_subgroup <- run_subgroup_trajectory(
>   merged_data_long,
>   "perceived_stress",
>   "perceived_stress_baseline_cat",
>   "Low baseline (PSS-4 < 8)"
> )
> stress_elevated_subgroup <- run_subgroup_trajectory(
>   merged_data_long,
>   "perceived_stress",
>   "perceived_stress_baseline_cat",
>   "Elevated baseline (PSS-4 ≥ 8)"
> )
> depression_low_subgroup <- run_subgroup_trajectory(
>   merged_data_long,
>   "depression",
>   "depression_baseline_cat",
>   "Low baseline (PHQ-2 < 3)"
> )
> depression_elevated_subgroup <- run_subgroup_trajectory(
>   merged_data_long,
>   "depression",
>   "depression_baseline_cat",
>   "Elevated baseline (PHQ-2 ≥ 3)"
> )
> anxiety_low_subgroup <- run_subgroup_trajectory(
>   merged_data_long,
>   "anxiety",
>   "anxiety_baseline_cat",
>   "Low baseline (GAD-2 < 3)"
> )
> anxiety_elevated_subgroup <- run_subgroup_trajectory(
>   merged_data_long,
>   "anxiety",
>   "anxiety_baseline_cat",
>   "Elevated baseline (GAD-2 ≥ 3)"
> )
> subgroup_interactions_raw <- bind_rows(
>   stress_low_subgroup$interaction,
>   stress_elevated_subgroup$interaction,
>   depression_low_subgroup$interaction,
>   depression_elevated_subgroup$interaction,
>   anxiety_low_subgroup$interaction,
>   anxiety_elevated_subgroup$interaction
> )
> subgroup_slopes_raw <- bind_rows(
>   stress_low_subgroup$slopes,
>   stress_elevated_subgroup$slopes,
>   depression_low_subgroup$slopes,
>   depression_elevated_subgroup$slopes,
>   anxiety_low_subgroup$slopes,
>   anxiety_elevated_subgroup$slopes
> )
> subgroup_timepoint_diffs_raw <- bind_rows(
>   stress_low_subgroup$timepoint_diffs,
>   stress_elevated_subgroup$timepoint_diffs,
>   depression_low_subgroup$timepoint_diffs,
>   depression_elevated_subgroup$timepoint_diffs,
>   anxiety_low_subgroup$timepoint_diffs,
>   anxiety_elevated_subgroup$timepoint_diffs
> )
> subgroup_week6_d_raw <- bind_rows(
>   stress_low_subgroup$week6_d,
>   stress_elevated_subgroup$week6_d,
>   depression_low_subgroup$week6_d,
>   depression_elevated_subgroup$week6_d,
>   anxiety_low_subgroup$week6_d,
>   anxiety_elevated_subgroup$week6_d
> )
> interaction_rows <- subgroup_interactions_raw |>
>   transmute(
>     outcome,
>     subgroup,
>     effect = "Condition × Time",
>     b,
>     SE,
>     t,
>     p,
>     standardized_effect = format_standardized_effect(
>       "β",
>       beta,
>       beta_LL,
>       beta_UL
>     )
>   )
> slope_rows <- subgroup_slopes_raw |>
>   transmute(
>     outcome,
>     subgroup,
>     effect = case_when(
>       cond == "Control" ~ "Time, control condition",
>       cond == "Flourish" ~ "Time, treatment condition",
>       TRUE ~ NA_character_
>     ),
>     b,
>     SE,
>     t,
>     p,
>     standardized_effect = format_standardized_effect(
>       "β",
>       beta,
>       beta_LL,
>       beta_UL
>     )
>   )
> week6_rows <- subgroup_timepoint_diffs_raw |>
>   filter(time_f == "Week 6") |>
>   left_join(
>     subgroup_week6_d_raw,
>     by = c("outcome", "subgroup")
>   ) |>
>   transmute(
>     outcome,
>     subgroup,
>     effect = "Week 6 treatment vs control",
>     b,
>     SE,
>     t,
>     p,
>     standardized_effect = format_standardized_effect(
>       "d",
>       d,
>       d_LL,
>       d_UL
>     )
>   )
> table2_subgroup_models <- bind_rows(
>   interaction_rows,
>   slope_rows,
>   week6_rows
> ) |>
>   mutate(
>     outcome_order = match(
>       outcome,
>       c("perceived_stress", "depression", "anxiety")
>     ),
> 
>     subgroup_order = if_else(
>       str_detect(subgroup, "<"),
>       1L,
>       2L
>     ),
> 
>     effect_order = match(
>       effect,
>       c(
>         "Condition × Time",
>         "Time, control condition",
>         "Time, treatment condition",
>         "Week 6 treatment vs control"
>       )
>     ),
> 
>     Outcome = recode(
>       outcome,
>       perceived_stress = "Perceived stress",
>       depression = "Depression",
>       anxiety = "Anxiety"
>     ),
> 
>     `Baseline severity` = case_when(
>       str_detect(subgroup, "PSS-4 < 8") ~ "Lower",
>       str_detect(subgroup, "PSS-4 ≥ 8") ~ "Higher",
>       str_detect(subgroup, "PHQ-2 < 3") ~ "Low",
>       str_detect(subgroup, "PHQ-2 ≥ 3") ~ "Elevated",
>       str_detect(subgroup, "GAD-2 < 3") ~ "Low",
>       str_detect(subgroup, "GAD-2 ≥ 3") ~ "Elevated",
>       TRUE ~ subgroup
>     ),
> 
>     b = round(b, 2),
>     SE = round(SE, 2),
>     t = round(t, 2),
>     `P value` = format_p(p)
>   ) |>
>   arrange(
>     outcome_order,
>     subgroup_order,
>     effect_order
>   ) |>
>   transmute(
>     Outcome,
>     `Baseline severity`,
>     Effect = effect,
>     b,
>     SE,
>     t,
>     `P value`,
>     `Standardized effect (95% CI)` = standardized_effect
>   )
> table2_subgroup_models |>
>   kable(
>     align = c("l", "l", "l", "r", "r", "r", "r", "l"),
>     caption = paste(
>       "Table 2. Mixed-effects model estimates for perceived stress,",
>       "depression, and anxiety by baseline severity group"
>     )
>   )

> change_data <- merged_data_long |>
>   filter(time %in% c(1, 4)) |>
>   select(
>     unique_ID,
>     cond,
>     time,
>     perceived_stress,
>     depression,
>     anxiety,
>     perceived_stress_baseline_cat,
>     depression_baseline_cat,
>     anxiety_baseline_cat
>   ) |>
>   pivot_wider(
>     names_from = time,
>     values_from = c(
>       depression,
>       anxiety,
>       perceived_stress
>     ),
>     names_prefix = "T"
>   ) |>
>   mutate(
>     depression_change =
>       depression_T1 - depression_T4,
> 
>     anxiety_change =
>       anxiety_T1 - anxiety_T4,
> 
>     perceived_stress_change =
>       perceived_stress_T1 - perceived_stress_T4
>   )
> calculate_within_condition_d <- function(
>     data,
>     pre_var,
>     post_var,
>     baseline_group_var,
>     outcome_label
> ) {
> 
>   data |>
>     filter(
>       !is.na(.data[[pre_var]]),
>       !is.na(.data[[post_var]]),
>       !is.na(.data[[baseline_group_var]]),
>       !is.na(cond)
>     ) |>
>     group_by(
>       baseline_group = .data[[baseline_group_var]],
>       cond
>     ) |>
>     group_modify(~ {
> 
>       es <- effectsize::repeated_measures_d(
>         x = .x[[pre_var]],
>         y = .x[[post_var]],
>         method = "av",
>         adjust = FALSE,
>         ci = 0.95
>       ) |>
>         as.data.frame()
> 
>       tibble(
>         n = nrow(.x),
> 
>         baseline_mean = mean(.x[[pre_var]]),
>         baseline_sd = sd(.x[[pre_var]]),
> 
>         week6_mean = mean(.x[[post_var]]),
>         week6_sd = sd(.x[[post_var]]),
> 
>         # Positive values indicate symptom improvement.
>         mean_change = mean(
>           .x[[pre_var]] - .x[[post_var]]
>         ),
> 
>         # The first column returned by repeated_measures_d()
>         # contains the requested effect-size estimate.
>         d_av = as.numeric(es[[1]][1]),
>         d_LL = as.numeric(es$CI_low[1]),
>         d_UL = as.numeric(es$CI_high[1])
>       )
>     }) |>
>     ungroup() |>
>     mutate(
>       outcome = outcome_label,
>       .before = 1
>     )
> }
> within_condition_d_results <- bind_rows(
> 
>   calculate_within_condition_d(
>     data = change_data,
>     pre_var = "perceived_stress_T1",
>     post_var = "perceived_stress_T4",
>     baseline_group_var = "perceived_stress_baseline_cat",
>     outcome_label = "Perceived stress"
>   ),
> 
>   calculate_within_condition_d(
>     data = change_data,
>     pre_var = "depression_T1",
>     post_var = "depression_T4",
>     baseline_group_var = "depression_baseline_cat",
>     outcome_label = "Depressive symptoms"
>   ),
> 
>   calculate_within_condition_d(
>     data = change_data,
>     pre_var = "anxiety_T1",
>     post_var = "anxiety_T4",
>     baseline_group_var = "anxiety_baseline_cat",
>     outcome_label = "Anxiety symptoms"
>   )
> ) |>
>   mutate(
>     outcome_order = match(
>       outcome,
>       c(
>         "Perceived stress",
>         "Depressive symptoms",
>         "Anxiety symptoms"
>       )
>     ),
> 
>     baseline_order = case_when(
>       str_detect(baseline_group, "Low baseline") ~ 1L,
>       str_detect(baseline_group, "Lower baseline") ~ 1L,
>       TRUE ~ 2L
>     ),
> 
>     condition_order = case_when(
>       cond == "Flourish" ~ 1L,
>       cond == "Control" ~ 2L,
>       TRUE ~ 3L
>     )
>   ) |>
>   arrange(
>     outcome_order,
>     baseline_order,
>     condition_order
>   )
> within_condition_d_results |>
>   mutate(
>     `Baseline, M (SD)` = sprintf(
>       "%.2f (%.2f)",
>       baseline_mean,
>       baseline_sd
>     ),
> 
>     `Week 6, M (SD)` = sprintf(
>       "%.2f (%.2f)",
>       week6_mean,
>       week6_sd
>     ),
> 
>     `Mean improvement` = sprintf(
>       "%.2f",
>       mean_change
>     ),
> 
>     `Within-condition d_av (95% CI)` = paste0(
>       format_ci_bound(d_av),
>       " (",
>       format_ci_bound(d_LL),
>       " to ",
>       format_ci_bound(d_UL),
>       ")"
>     )
>   ) |>
>   select(
>     Outcome = outcome,
>     `Baseline group` = baseline_group,
>     Condition = cond,
>     n,
>     `Baseline, M (SD)`,
>     `Week 6, M (SD)`,
>     `Mean improvement`,
>     `Within-condition d_av (95% CI)`
>   ) |>
>   kable(
>     align = c("l", "l", "l", "r", "c", "c", "c", "c"),
>     caption = paste(
>       "Within-condition standardized mean change from",
>       "baseline to Week 6 by baseline severity group"
>     )
>   )

> run_meaningful_change <- function(
>     data,
>     outcome_change_var,
>     baseline_cat_var,
>     elevated_label,
>     missing_nonresponse = FALSE
> ) {
>   
>   d <- data |>
>     filter(.data[[baseline_cat_var]] == elevated_label) |>
>     mutate(cond = factor(cond, levels = c("Control", "Flourish")))
> 
>   if (!missing_nonresponse) {
>     d <- d |>
>       filter(!is.na(.data[[outcome_change_var]])) |>
>       mutate(responder = .data[[outcome_change_var]] >= 2)
>   } else {
>     d <- d |>
>       mutate(
>         responder = !is.na(.data[[outcome_change_var]]) &
>           .data[[outcome_change_var]] >= 2
>       )
>   }
> 
>   responders <- d |>
>     group_by(cond) |>
>     summarise(
>       n = n(),
>       n_responder = sum(responder),
>       pct_responder = 100 * mean(responder),
>       .groups = "drop"
>     )
> 
>   fit <- glm(
>     responder ~ cond,
>     data = d,
>     family = binomial
>   )
> 
>   fit_null <- glm(
>     responder ~ 1,
>     data = d,
>     family = binomial
>   )
> 
>   p <- anova(
>     fit_null,
>     fit,
>     test = "LRT"
>   )$`Pr(>Chi)`[2]
> 
>   flourish_n <- responders |>
>     filter(cond == "Flourish") |>
>     pull(n)
> 
>   flourish_resp <- responders |>
>     filter(cond == "Flourish") |>
>     pull(n_responder)
> 
>   control_n <- responders |>
>     filter(cond == "Control") |>
>     pull(n)
> 
>   control_resp <- responders |>
>     filter(cond == "Control") |>
>     pull(n_responder)
> 
>   flourish_rate <- flourish_resp / flourish_n
>   control_rate <- control_resp / control_n
> 
>   # Risk ratio and 95% CI
>   rr <- flourish_rate / control_rate
> 
>   se_log_rr <- sqrt(
>     (1 / flourish_resp) - (1 / flourish_n) +
>       (1 / control_resp) - (1 / control_n)
>   )
> 
>   rr_ci <- exp(
>     log(rr) + c(-1, 1) * 1.96 * se_log_rr
>   )
> 
>   # Absolute risk reduction and 95% CI
>   arr <- flourish_rate - control_rate
> 
>   se_arr <- sqrt(
>     flourish_rate * (1 - flourish_rate) / flourish_n +
>       control_rate * (1 - control_rate) / control_n
>   )
> 
>   arr_ci <- arr + c(-1, 1) * 1.96 * se_arr
> 
>   # NNT and 95% CI
>   # The NNT CI is obtained by inverting the ARR CI.
>   # Bounds reverse when inverted.
>   nnt <- ifelse(arr > 0, 1 / arr, NA_real_)
> 
>   nnt_ci <- if (arr_ci[1] > 0 & arr_ci[2] > 0) {
>     c(1 / arr_ci[2], 1 / arr_ci[1])
>   } else {
>     c(NA_real_, NA_real_)
>   }
> 
>   list(
>     responders = responders,
>     RR = unname(rr),
>     RR_CI_low = unname(rr_ci[1]),
>     RR_CI_high = unname(rr_ci[2]),
>     p = p,
>     ARR = unname(arr),
>     ARR_CI_low = unname(arr_ci[1]),
>     ARR_CI_high = unname(arr_ci[2]),
>     NNT = unname(nnt),
>     NNT_CI_low = unname(nnt_ci[1]),
>     NNT_CI_high = unname(nnt_ci[2])
>   )
> }
> depression_meaningful <- run_meaningful_change(
>   change_data,
>   "depression_change",
>   "depression_baseline_cat",
>   "Elevated baseline (PHQ-2 ≥ 3)"
> )
> anxiety_meaningful <- run_meaningful_change(
>   change_data,
>   "anxiety_change",
>   "anxiety_baseline_cat",
>   "Elevated baseline (GAD-2 ≥ 3)"
> )

> format_nnt <- function(x) {
>   if (is.na(x$NNT)) {
>     return(NA_character_)
>   }
>   
>   if (is.na(x$NNT_CI_low) | is.na(x$NNT_CI_high)) {
>     return(sprintf("%.1f [not estimable]", abs(x$NNT)))
>   }
>   
>   sprintf(
>     "%.1f [%.1f, %.1f]",
>     abs(x$NNT),
>     abs(x$NNT_CI_low),
>     abs(x$NNT_CI_high)
>   )
> }
> format_meaningful <- function(x, outcome, analysis) {
>   
>   r <- x$responders
> 
>   tibble(
>     Outcome = outcome,
>     Analysis = analysis,
>     Flourish = sprintf(
>       "%d/%d (%.1f%%)",
>       r$n_responder[r$cond == "Flourish"],
>       r$n[r$cond == "Flourish"],
>       r$pct_responder[r$cond == "Flourish"]
>     ),
>     Control = sprintf(
>       "%d/%d (%.1f%%)",
>       r$n_responder[r$cond == "Control"],
>       r$n[r$cond == "Control"],
>       r$pct_responder[r$cond == "Control"]
>     ),
>     RR = sprintf("%.2f", x$RR),
>     `RR 95% CI` = sprintf(
>       "[%.2f, %.2f]",
>       x$RR_CI_low,
>       x$RR_CI_high
>     ),
>     p = format_p(x$p),
>     ARR = sprintf("%.1f", 100 * x$ARR),
>     `ARR 95% CI` = sprintf(
>       "[%.1f, %.1f]",
>       100 * x$ARR_CI_low,
>       100 * x$ARR_CI_high
>     ),
>     NNT = format_nnt(x)
>   )
> }

> plot_trajectory <- function(
>     df,
>     outcome,
>     baseline_cat,
>     y_label,
>     output_file,
>     y_breaks = 1:4,
>     y_limits = c(0.8, 4.7)

> 
>   plot_df <- df |>

>       !is.na(.data[[outcome]]),

> 
>         as.character(.data[[baseline_cat]]) %in%
>           c(

>             "Elevated baseline (PHQ-2 ≥ 3)",
>             "Elevated baseline (GAD-2 ≥ 3)"
>           ) ~ "High Baseline",
> 
>         TRUE ~ as.character(.data[[baseline_cat]])
>       ),
>       baseline_cat_plot = factor(
>         baseline_cat_plot,
>         levels = c("Low Baseline", "High Baseline")

>       )

> ri_lavaan_data <- ri_data |>
>   select(-unique_ID)
> ri_clpm_model <- '
> 
> # BETWEEN-PERSON RANDOM INTERCEPTS
> 
> RI_stress =~
>   1*perceived_stress_t1 +
>   1*perceived_stress_t2 +
>   1*perceived_stress_t3 +
>   1*perceived_stress_t4
> 
> RI_dep =~
>   1*depression_t1 +
>   1*depression_t2 +
>   1*depression_t3 +
>   1*depression_t4
> 
> RI_anx =~
>   1*anxiety_t1 +
>   1*anxiety_t2 +
>   1*anxiety_t3 +
>   1*anxiety_t4
> 
> RI_stress ~~ RI_dep
> RI_stress ~~ RI_anx
> RI_dep ~~ RI_anx
> 
> 
> # WITHIN-PERSON COMPONENTS
>

Ethics Approval Check

Based on the following text, we would expect an ethics approval statement, and it was present:

In this preregistered trial, 1,137 unscreened university students were assigned to 6 weeks of a generative AIpowered mobile intervention (“Flourish”) or care as usual.

All participants provided informed consent prior to participation.

A total of 1137 undergraduate students were recruited through a large North American university’s Sona research participation system during the summer and fall 2025 academic terms in exchange for research credit.

Participants were required to be at least 18 years old; no other eligibility criteria were applied.

Participants were randomized in a 1:1 ratio through Qualtrics’ built-in randomizer at the end of the baseline survey to either the treatment condition or a care-as-usual control condition in a parallel-group superiority design.

Participants assigned to treatment received access to the Flourish app and were instructed to use it at least twice weekly for 6 weeks; however, research credit was awarded based on survey completion, independent of app engagement.

Participants assigned to the control condition did not receive access to Flourish during the study period and retained access to usual campus or personal mental health resources.

Intervention: The Flourish Platform Participants assigned to the intervention received access to Flourish, a self-guided digital mental health app available on iOS and Android.

All randomized participants were analyzed under an intention-to-treat approach to preserve the benefits of randomization.

A total of 1,137 participants were enrolled in the study between May and November 2025 and randomized to either the treatment condition (n = 556) or care-as-usual (n = 581).

By Week 6, participants assigned to treatment had transcribed an average of 18.9 app-tracked active days (SD = 10.2), exceeding the instructed minimum of twice weekly (12 days total).

Participants assigned to use Flourish twice weekly showed more favorable trajectories across all three outcomes than those assigned to care as usual, with lower perceived stress, depressive symptoms, and anxiety symptoms by Week 6.

Ethics approval statement:

The trial was prospectively registered on ClinicalTrials.gov on October 29, 2024 (NCT06668532), and approved by the university Research Ethics Board (REB; HE2025-0023).

During the study period, no adverse events attributable to the intervention were reported to the study investigators or Research Ethics Board, and none were identified through review of open-ended responses collected across survey waves.

Power Analysis Check

No power analyses were detected.

Power analyses need to contain the following information to be interpretable: the type of power analysis, the statistical test, the software used, sample size, critical alpha criterion, power level, effect size, and an effect size metric. In addition, it is recommended to make sure the power analysis is reproducible (by sharing the code, or a screenshot, of the power analysis), and to provide good arguments for why the study was designed to detect an effect of this size.

For an a-priori power analysis, where the sample size is determined, reporting all information would look like:

An a priori power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that for a Cohen’s d = 0.5, an alpha level of 0.05, and a desired power level of 80% required at least 64 participants in each group.

For a sensitivity power analysis, this sentence would look like:

A sensitivity power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that with 64 participants in each group, and an alpha level of 0.05, a desired power level of 80% was reached for an effect size of d = 0.5.

Preregistration Check

We found 1 preregistration.

id title template
yrw2-szwp Multi-Institutional Study on Student Flourishing AsPredicted

Meta-scientific research has shown that deviations from preregistrations are often not reported or checked, and that the most common deviations concern the sample size. We recommend manually checking the full preregistration at the link above, and have provided the preregistered sample size.

id sample_size
yrw2-szwp The sample size will be determined by the number of students who opt into the study for extra or research credit at each institution, with no minimum set but aiming to collect as many participants as possible.
Field Preregistration 1
template_name AsPredicted
id yrw2-szwp
link https://aspredicted.org/yrw2-szwp.pdf
title Multi-Institutional Study on Student Flourishing
date_created 2024/10/21 10:32 (PT)
date_registered 2024-10-21
existing_data_explanation It’s complicated. We have already collected some data but explain in Question 8 why readers may consider this a valid pre-registration nevertheless.
research_questions Does the use of a digital well-being toolkit (the Flourish app) improve well-being among students over a 6-week period compared to a control group?
design_dependent_variables Primary outcomes:
Flourishing Scale (Diener et al., 2009), depression (PHQ-2), anxiety (GAD-2), perceived stress (PSS-4; Cohen et al., 1983), affect (Subcomponents of Affect Scale), emotional resilience (Smith et al., 2008)

Secondary outcomes:
Loneliness (UCLA 3-item Loneliness Scale), belonging (Walton & Cohen, 2007; Bollen & Hoyle, 1990), actual and ideal affect (Tsai et al., 2006), expectations and experiences of chatbots (adapted from WAI-SR); trust (Ullman & Malle, 2023), academic self-efficacy (van Zyl et al., 2022), overall school perceptions
study_design_overview Students will be randomly assigned to one of two conditions: 1) the intervention group, where they will have access to the Flourish app, a digital well-being toolkit, and will be instructed to use the app twice a week over the 6-week period, and 2) the control group, where participants will not receive any intervention and will only complete baseline and follow-up assessments during the 6 weeks.
statistical_tests The study will use longitudinal linear mixed-effects models to analyze outcomes across 4 timepoints (baseline, week 2, week 4, and week 6), with institution and participant included as random effects. The primary predictors will be the Time x Condition interaction to assess whether our outcome variables of interest (e.g., emotional resilience) improve more in the Flourish condition compared to the control condition over time.
outliers_and_exclusions Participants who fail to complete a minimum of 2 timepoints will be excluded from the final analysis. Additionally, students in the treatment condition who do not use the Flourish app, as well as those in the control condition who gain access to the app will also be excluded.
sample_size The sample size will be determined by the number of students who opt into the study for extra or research credit at each institution, with no minimum set but aiming to collect as many participants as possible.
additional_comments We will conduct exploratory analyses to examine the association between engagement with the Flourish app and well-being outcomes within the intervention group. We will test whether higher levels of engagement are associated with greater improvements in mental health outcomes.

In addition, we will explore whether engagement levels and the effects of the digital toolkit are moderated by demographic factors such as age, gender, and baseline level of well-being/anxiety/loneliness etc. These analyses will help identify whether the intervention is more effective for specific subgroups over others.

Addressing Question 1: Some data have already been collected (we are a third of the way into the 6-week study) as we needed to launch in alignment with the academic semester schedule. However, we have not accessed or reviewed any of the data.

Additional author: Smaranda Lawrie, Providence College (slawrie@providence.edu)
