Fig 1 :

Fig 1: Architecture of the agentic AI system underlying the "Chat with Sunnie" feature in the Flourish

Among participants with observed scores at both baseline and Week 6, we calculated within-condition standardized mean changes separately by condition and baseline-severity group. The mean baseline-to-Week 6 change was standardized using the square root of the average of the baseline and Week 6 variances. Effect sizes were oriented so that positive values indicated improvement and negative values indicated worsening. Meaningful-change analyses were limited to depressive and anxiety symptoms and to participants with elevated baseline PHQ-2 or GAD-2 scores and observed Week 6 outcome data. Because these are brief screening measures with 0-6 score ranges, meaningful change was defined as a reduction of at least 2 points from baseline to Week 6. Under linear rescaling, this corresponds to approximately a 9-point reduction on the PHQ-9 and a 7-point reduction on the GAD-7, exceeding established minimally

Fig 2 :

Fig 2: CONSORT flow diagram.

Fig 3 :

Fig 3: Trajectories of perceived stress, depressive symptoms, and anxiety symptoms by condition and baseline severity.

Fig 4 :

Fig 4: Random-intercept cross-lagged panel model of perceived stress, depressive symptoms, and 05 **p<.01 ***p<.001 All 5,000 bootstrap resamples converged and yielded admissible solutions.

The focal indirect effects were consistent with an indirect pathway from treatment through lower perceived stress at Week 4 to lower depressive symptoms at Week 6 (indirect effect = -0.027; 95% percentile bootstrap CI [-0.056, -0.004]) and lower anxiety symptoms at Week 6 (-0.018; 95% CI [-0.044, -0.0003]). Earlier Week 2 and serial stress pathways had confidence intervals that included zero (Supplementary Note 7). Across two sensitivity analyses, the focal indirect effects remained negative; confidence intervals excluded zero in the conventional CLPM and narrowly included zero when treatment was additionally allowed to predict the RI-CLPM random intercepts (Supplementary Note 7).

Sixth, concurrent psychotherapy and medication use were not recorded, limiting characterization of the care context and comparison of standalone versus adjunctive use. Finally, the stress pathway analyses were post hoc. Although random-intercept decomposition estimates within-person indirect effects net of stable between-person differences, it cannot exclude time-varying confounding of the stress-symptom association (e.g., sleep or behavioral activation); use of brief observed composites and conceptual overlap among stress, depression, and anxiety may also complicate interpretation of cross-lagged associations.These analyses support perceived stress as a potential mechanism but do not establish a causal stresssymptom pathway.Future research should examine the durability and generalizability of symptom improvements and identify which interventions work best for whom and when, including through just-in-time adaptive interventions (JITAIs). Future studies should also causally test the stress-symptom pathway, using approaches such as denser longitudinal assessments and experimental manipulation of stress-focused components. At the health-system level, beyond standalone self-guided use, purpose-built AI should be evaluated within stepped-care, blended-care, and therapy-companion models,66 including whether benefits persist or increase when AI complements ongoing treatment and how such systems can flexibly support individuals before, during, and after clinical care.Together, these findings provide randomized evidence that a purpose-built, clinician-overseen AI platform can improve trajectories of perceived stress, depressive symptoms, and anxiety symptoms in an unscreened population, across levels of baseline severity. They also point to perceived stress as a potential pathway to broader benefits. By combining the evidence base and structure of self-guided digital interventions with the flexibility and engagement of conversational AI, such platforms may offer scalable first-line support spanning prevention and early intervention.
